<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="200.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">COMP[1:0]</span>: Companding mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. The µ-Law and the A-Law log are a part of the CCITT G.711 recommendation, the type of complement that is used depends on <span style="font-style:Italic;">CPL bit</span>.</p><p class="TableIndent">The data expansion or data compression are determined by the state of bit MODE[0]. </p><p class="TableIndent">The data compression is applied if the audio block is configured as a transmitter.</p><p class="TableIndent">The data expansion is automatically applied when the audio block is configured as a receiver.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_49327__1_13_3_Companding_Mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Companding mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: No companding algorithm</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Reserved.</p><p class="TableIndent">10: µ-Law algorithm</p><p class="TableIndent">11: A-Law algorithm</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Companding mode is applicable only when Free protocol mode is selected.</p></td></tr><tr height="105.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">CPL</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Complement bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">It defines the type of complement to be used for companding mode </p><p class="TableIndent">0: 1’s complement representation.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: 2’s complement representation.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit has effect only when the companding mode is µ-Law algorithm or A-Law algorithm.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 12:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTECNT[5:0]</span>: Mute counter<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They are used only in reception mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">The value set in these bits is compared to the number of consecutive mute frames detected in reception. When the number of mute frames is equal to this value, the flag MUTEDET is set and an interrupt is generated if bit MUTEDETIE is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_54170__1_13_1_Mute_mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mute mode</a> for more details.</p></td></tr><tr height="181.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTEVAL</span>: Mute value.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only when the audio block operates as a transmitter, the number of slots is lower or equal to 2 and the MUTE bit is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">If more slots are declared, the bit value sent during the transmission in mute mode is equal to 0, whatever the value of MUTEVAL.</p><p class="TableIndent">if the number of slot is lower or equal to 2 and MUTEVAL = 1, the MUTE value transmitted for each slot is the one sent during the previous frame.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_54170__1_13_1_Mute_mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mute mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Bit value 0 is sent during the mute mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Last values are sent during the mute mode.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">This bit is meaningless and must not be used for SPDIF audio blocks.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTE:</span> Mute.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only when the audio block operates as a transmitter. The MUTE value is linked to value of MUTEVAL if the number of slots is lower or equal to 2, or equal to 0 if it is greater than 2.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_54170__1_13_1_Mute_mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mute mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No mute mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Mute mode enabled.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">This bit is meaningless and must not be used for SPDIF audio blocks.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="132.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TRIS</span>: Tristate management on data line. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only if the audio block is configured as a transmitter. <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">This bit is not used when the audio block is configured in SPDIF mode. </span>It must be configured when SAI is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_50294__1_13_4_Ouput_data_line_management_on_an_inactive_slot20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Output data line management on an inactive slot</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SD output line is still driven by the SAI when a slot is inactive.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: SD output line is released (HI-Z) at the end of the last data bit of the last active slot if the next one is inactive.</p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FFLUSH:</span> FIFO flush.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software. It is always read as 0. This bit must be configured when the SAI is disabled. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: No FIFO flush. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: FIFO flush. Programming this bit to 1 triggers the FIFO Flush. All the internal FIFO pointers (read and write) are cleared. In this case data still present in the FIFO are lost (no more transmission or received data lost). Before flushing, SAI DMA stream/interrupt must be disabled</p></td></tr><tr height="168.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FTH[2:0]:</span> FIFO threshold. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">000: FIFO empty </p><p class="TableIndent">001: ¼ FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">010: ½ FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">011: ¾ FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">100: FIFO full</p><p class="TableIndent">101: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">110: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">111: Reserved</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.3 SAI frame configuration register (SAI\_AFRCR)

Address offset: 0x0C

Reset value: 0x0000 0007

**Note:**

This register has no meaning in AC’97 and SPDIF audio protocol.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.07px"/><col width="539.22px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSOFF</span>: Frame synchronization offset. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningless and is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration. This bit must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FS is asserted on the first bit of the slot 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FS is asserted one bit before the first bit of the slot 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSPOL</span>: Frame synchronization polarity. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is used to configure the level of the start of frame on the FS signal. It is meaningless and is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FS is active low (falling edge)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FS is active high (rising edge)</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSDEF</span>: Frame synchronization definition.</p><p class="TableBodyL">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FS signal is a start frame signal</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FS signal is a start of frame signal + channel side identification</p><p class="TableIndent">When the bit is set, the number of slots defined in the SAI_xSLOTR register has to be even. It means that half of this number of slots are dedicated to the left channel and the other slots for the right channel (e.g: this bit has to be set for I2S or MSB/LSB-justified protocols...). </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is meaningless and is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration. It must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 14:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSALL[6:0]</span>: Frame synchronization active level length. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They specify the length in number of bit clock <br/>(SCK) + 1 (FSALL[6:0] + 1) of the active level of the FS signal in the audio frame </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are meaningless and are not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration.</p><p class="TableIndent">They must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="176.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FRL[7:0]</span>: Frame length. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They define the audio frame length expressed in number of SCK clock cycles: the number of bits in the frame is equal to FRL[7:0] + 1. </p><p class="TableIndent">The minimum number of bits to transfer in an audio frame must be equal to 8, otherwise the audio block behaves in an unexpected way. This is the case when the data size is 8 bits and only one slot 0 is defined in NBSLOT[4:0] of SAI_xSLOTR register (NBSLOT[3:0] = 0000).</p><p class="TableIndent">In master mode, if the master clock (available on MCLK_x pin) is used, the frame length must be aligned with a number equal to a power of 2, ranging from 8 to 256. When the master clock is not used (<span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3">NODIV</span> = 1), it is recommended to program the frame length to an value ranging from 8 to 256.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are meaningless and are not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration. They must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.4 SAI slot register (SAI\_ASLOTR)

Address offset: 0x10

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

**Note:**

This register has no meaning in AC’97 and SPDIF audio protocol.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SLOTEN[15:0]</span>: Slot enable. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">Each SLOTEN bit corresponds to a slot position from 0 to 15 (maximum 16 slots).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Inactive slot.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Active slot.</p><p class="TableIndent">The slot must be enabled when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="182.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NBSLOT[3:0]</span>: Number of slots in an audio frame. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">The value set in this bitfield represents the number of slots in the audio frame (including the number of inactive slots). The maximum number of slots is 16.</p><p class="TableIndent">The number of slots must be even if FSDEF bit in the SAI_xFRCR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">The number of slots must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: Number of slots is 1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0001: Number of slots is 2.</p><p class="TableIndent">...</p><p class="TableIndent">1111: Number of slots is 16.</p></td></tr><tr height="182.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SLOTSZ[1:0]</span>: Slot size</p><p class="TableIndent">This bits is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">The slot size must be higher or equal to the data size. If this condition is not respected, the behavior of the SAI is undetermined. </p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_50294__1_13_4_Ouput_data_line_management_on_an_inactive_slot20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Output data line management on an inactive slot</a> for information on how to drive SD line. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits must be set when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: The slot size is equivalent to the data size (specified in DS[3:0] in the SAI_xCR1 register).</p><p class="TableIndent">01: 16-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">10: 32-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Reserved</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 4:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FBOFF[4:0]</span>: First bit offset<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span></p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">The value set in this bitfield defines the position of the first data transfer bit in the slot. It represents an offset value. In transmission mode, the bits outside the data field are forced to 0. In reception mode, the extra received bits are discarded.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits must be set when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.5 SAI interrupt mask register (SAI\_AIM)

Address offset: 0x14

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LFSDETIE</span>: Late frame synchronization detection interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the LFSDET bit is set in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is meaningless in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">, SPDIF</span> mode or when the audio block operates as a master.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">AFSDETIE</span>: Anticipated frame synchronization detection interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the AFSDET bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is meaningless in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">, SPDIF</span> mode or when the audio block operates as a master.</p></td></tr><tr height="148.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNRDYIE</span>: Codec not ready interrupt enable (AC’97). </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When the interrupt is enabled, the audio block detects in the slot 0 (tag0) of the AC’97 frame if the Codec connected to this line is ready or not. If it is not ready, the CNRDY flag in the SAI_xSR register is set and an interrupt is generated.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit has a meaning only if the AC’97 mode is selected through PRTCFG[1:0] bits and the audio block is operates as a receiver.</p></td></tr><tr height="118.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FREQIE</span>: FIFO request interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the FREQ bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">Since the audio block defaults to operate as a transmitter after reset, the MODE bit must be configured before setting FREQIE to avoid a parasitic interrupt in receiver mode,</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WCKCFGIE</span>: Wrong clock configuration interrupt enable.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is taken into account only if the audio block is configured as a master (MODE[1] = 0) and <span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3,rm0539_mustang">NODIV</span> = 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">It generates an interrupt if the WCKCFG flag in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is used only in <span style="font-style:normal;">Free protocol mode</span> and is meaningless in other modes.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTEDETIE</span>: Mute detection interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">. </span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the MUTEDET bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit has a meaning only if the audio block is configured in receiver mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVRUDRIE</span>: Overrun/underrun interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">. </span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the OVRUDR bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.6 SAI status register (SAI\_ASR)

Address offset: 0x18

Reset value: 0x0000 0008

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="166.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 18:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">FLVL[2:0]</span>: FIFO level threshold. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only. The FIFO level threshold flag is managed only by hardware and its setting depends on SAI block configuration (transmitter or receiver mode). </p><p class="TableIndent">000: FIFO empty (transmitter and receiver modes)</p><p class="TableIndent">001: FIFO ≤ ¼ but not empty (transmitter mode), FIFO &lt; ¼ but not empty (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">010: ¼ &lt; FIFO ≤ ½ (transmitter mode), ¼ ≤ FIFO &lt; ½ (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">011: ½ &lt; FIFO ≤ ¾ (transmitter mode), ½ ≤ FIFO &lt; ¾ (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">100: ¾ &lt; FIFO but not full (transmitter mode), ¾ ≤ FIFO but not full (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">101: FIFO full (transmitter and receiver modes)</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: Reserved </p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LFSDET</span>: Late frame synchronization detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No error.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Frame synchronization signal is not present at the right time. </p><p class="TableIndent">This flag can be set only if the audio block is configured in slave mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if LFSDETIE bit is set in the SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets bit CLFSDET in SAI_xCLRFR register</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">AFSDET</span>: Anticipated frame synchronization detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No error.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Frame synchronization signal is detected earlier than expected. </p><p class="TableIndent">This flag can be set only if the audio block is configured in slave mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It is not used in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang"> or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if AFSDETIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets CAFSDET bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNRDY</span>: Codec not ready. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: External AC’97 Codec is ready </p><p class="TableIndent">1: External AC’97 Codec is not ready</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used only when the AC’97 audio protocol is selected in the SAI_xCR1 register and configured in receiver mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if CNRDYIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets CCNRDY bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="169.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FREQ</span>: FIFO request. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No FIFO request.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FIFO request to read or to write the SAI_xDR.</p><p class="TableIndent">The request depends on the audio block configuration:</p><li class="TableBullet" id="16790240">–<span style="padding-right:3pt ;">​</span>If the block is configured in transmission mode, the FIFO request is related to a write request operation in the SAI_xDR. </li><li class="TableBullet" id="16790241">–<span style="padding-right:3pt ;">​</span>If the block configured in reception, the FIFO request related to a read request operation from the SAI_xDR.</li><p class="TableIndent">This flag can generate an interrupt if FREQIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WCKCFG</span>: Wrong clock configuration flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Clock configuration is correct</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Clock configuration does not respect the rule concerning the frame length specification defined in <a href="#XREF_85534__1_7_Frame_synchronization20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 43.4.6: Frame synchronization</a> (configuration of FRL[7:0] bit in the SAI_xFRCR register)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used only when the audio block operates in master mode (MODE[1] = 0) and <span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3,rm0539_mustang">NODIV</span> = 0. </p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if WCKCFGIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets CWCKCFG bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTEDET</span>: Mute detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No MUTE detection on the SD input line</p><p class="TableIndent">1: MUTE value detected on the SD input line (0 value) for a specified number of consecutive audio frame</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set if consecutive 0 values are received in each slot of a given audio frame and for a consecutive number of audio frames (set in the MUTECNT bit in the SAI_xCR2 register).</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if MUTEDETIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets bit CMUTEDET in the SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVRUDR</span>: Overrun / underrun.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No overrun/underrun error.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Overrun/underrun error detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">The overrun and underrun conditions can occur only when the audio block is configured as a receiver and a transmitter, respectively.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if OVRUDRIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets COVRUDR bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.7 SAI clear flag register (SAI\_ACLRFR)

Address offset: 0x1C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CLFSDET</span>: Clear late frame synchronization detection flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is write only.</p><p class="TableIndent">Programming this bit to 1 clears the LFSDET flag in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is not used in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang"> or SPDIF </span>mode</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading this bit always returns the value 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CAFSDET</span>: Clear anticipated frame synchronization detection flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is write only.</p><p class="TableIndent">Programming this bit to 1 clears the AFSDET flag in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">It is not used in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang"> or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading this bit always returns the value 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CCNRDY</span>: Clear Codec not ready flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is write only.</p><p class="TableIndent">Programming this bit to 1 clears the CNRDY flag in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used only when the AC’97 audio protocol is selected in the SAI_xCR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading this bit always returns the value 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CWCKCFG</span>: Clear wrong clock configuration flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is write only.</p><p class="TableIndent">Programming this bit to 1 clears the WCKCFG flag in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used only when the audio block is set as master (MODE[1] = 0) and <span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3,rm0539_mustang">NODIV</span> = 0 in the SAI_xCR1 register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading this bit always returns the value 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CMUTEDET</span>: Mute detection flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is write only.</p><p class="TableIndent">Programming this bit to 1 clears the MUTEDET flag in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading this bit always returns the value 0. </p></td></tr><tr height="72.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">COVRUDR</span>: Clear overrun / underrun.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is write only.</p><p class="TableIndent">Programming this bit to 1 clears the OVRUDR flag in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">Reading this bit always returns the value 0.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.8 SAI data register (SAI\_ADR)

Address offset: 0x20

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:<span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3">28</span></p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3" height="56.00px"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 27</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MCKEN:</span> Master clock generation enable</p><p class="TableIndent">0: The master clock is not generated</p><p class="TableIndent">1: The master clock is generated independently of SAIEN bit</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 26</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OSR:</span> Oversampling ratio for master clock</p><p class="TableIndent"><span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3">This bit is meaningful only when NODIV bit is set to 0.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Master clock frequency = F<sub>FS</sub> x 256</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Master clock frequency = F<sub>FS</sub> x 512</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3" height="104.00px"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 25:20</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MCKDIV[5:0]</span>: <span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Master clock divider</span></p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">000000: Divides by 1 the kernel clock input (sai_x_ker_ck).</p><p class="TableIndent">Otherwise, the master clock frequency is calculated according to the formula given in <span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2,_sai1_ckgen3"><a href="#XREF_56578__43_4_7_SAI_clock_generator20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 43.4.8</a></span>.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits have no meaning when the audio block is slave.</p><p class="TableIndent">They have to be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1" height="132.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 23:20</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL">MCKDIV[3:0]: <span class="Italic" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Master clock divider</span></p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. These bits are meaningless when the audio block operates in slave mode. They have to be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: Divides by 1 the master clock input.</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: the master clock frequency is calculated accordingly to the following formula:<span style="font-size : 10.00pt;"><img alt="RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_sai1_UserSpec_image1489" class="imageSettings" height="46.000224" src="RM0440_STM32G4xx_c7amba_sai1_UserSpec_image1489.png" style="display:block;margin:auto;" width="538.487305"/></span></p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3" height="72.00px"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen3"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NODIV:</span> No divider</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: the ratio between the Master clock generator and frame synchronization is fixed to 256 or 512</p><p class="TableIndent">1: the ratio between the Master clock generator and frame synchronization depends on FRL[7:0]</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1" height="72.00px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NODIV:</span> No divider</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Master clock generator is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: No divider used in the clock generator (in this case Master Clock Divider bit has no effect)</p></td></tr><tr data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2" height="86.67px" style="display: none"><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2" style="display: none"><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 19</p></td><td data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen2" style="display: none"><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NOMCK:</span> No divider</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Master clock generator is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Master clock generator is disabled. The clock divider controlled by MCKDIV can still be used to generate the bit clock.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DMAEN</span>: DMA enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: DMA disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: DMA enabled</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Since the audio block defaults to operate as a transmitter after reset, the MODE[1:0] bits must be configured before setting DMAEN to avoid a DMA request in receiver mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="194.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SAIEN</span>: Audio block enable</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">To switch off the audio block, the application software must program this bit to 0 and poll the bit till it reads back 0, meaning that the block is completely disabled. Before setting this bit to 1, check that it is set to 0, otherwise the enable command is not taken into account. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit enables to control the state of the SAI audio block. If it is disabled when an audio frame transfer is ongoing, the ongoing transfer completes and the cell is fully disabled at the end of this audio frame transfer.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SAI audio block disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: SAI audio block enabled. </p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>When the SAI block (A or B) is configured in master mode, the clock must be present on the SAI block input before setting SAIEN bit.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="89.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OUTDRIV</span>: Output drive</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Audio block output driven when SAIEN is set</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Audio block output driven immediately after the setting of this bit.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit has to be set before enabling the audio block and after the audio block configuration. </p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MONO</span>: Mono mode</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only when the number of slots is equal to 2. When the mono mode is selected, slot 0 data are duplicated on slot 1 when the audio block operates as a transmitter. In reception mode, the slot1 is discarded and only the data received from slot 0 are stored. Refer to <a href="#XREF_11395__1_13_2_MONO/STEREO_function20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mono/stereo mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Stereo mode</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Mono mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="150.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:10</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SYNCEN[1:0]:</span> Synchronization enable</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They must be configured when the audio subblock is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: audio subblock in asynchronous mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">01: audio subblock is synchronous with the other internal audio subblock. In this case, the audio subblock must be configured in slave mode</p><p class="TableIndent">10: <span data-conditional="_sai1_nosync">Reserved</span>.</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Reserved</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">The audio subblock must be configured as asynchronous when SPDIF mode is enabled.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 9</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CKSTR:</span> Clock strobing edge</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It must be configured when the audio block is disabled. <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">This bit has no meaning in SPDIF audio protocol.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Signals generated by the SAI change on SCK rising edge, while signals received by the SAI are sampled on the SCK falling edge.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Signals generated by the SAI change on SCK falling edge, while signals received by the SAI are sampled on the SCK rising edge.</p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LSBFIRST:</span> Least significant bit first</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It must be configured when the audio block is disabled. This bit has no meaning in AC’97 audio protocol since AC’97 data are always transferred with the MSB first. <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">This bit has no meaning in SPDIF audio protocol since in SPDIF data are always transferred with LSB first.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">0: Data are transferred with MSB first</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Data are transferred with LSB first</p></td></tr><tr height="228.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">DS[2:0]:</span> Data size</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">These bits are ignored when the SPDIF protocols are selected (bit PRTCFG[1:0]), because the frame and the data size are fixed in such case. </span>When the companding mode is selected through COMP[1:0] bits, DS[1:0] are ignored since the data size is fixed to 8 bits by the algorithm.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">000: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">001: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">010: 8 bits</p><p class="TableIndent">011: 10 bits</p><p class="TableIndent">100: 16 bits</p><p class="TableIndent">101: 20 bits</p><p class="TableIndent">110: 24 bits</p><p class="TableIndent">111: 32 bits</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="148.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 3:2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">PRTCFG[1:0]:</span> Protocol configuration</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. These bits have to be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: Free protocol. Free protocol enables to use the powerful config uration of the audio block to address a specific audio protocol (such as I2S, LSB/MSB justified, TDM, PCM/DSP...) by setting most of the configuration register bits as well as frame configuration register. </p><p class="TableIndent">01: <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">SPDIF protocol</span></p><p class="TableIndent">10: AC’97 protocol</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Reserved</p></td></tr><tr height="165.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 1:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MODE[1:0]:</span> SAIx audio block mode</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They must be configured when SAIx audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: Master transmitter</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Master receiver</p><p class="TableIndent">10: Slave transmitter</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Slave receiver</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">When the audio block is configured in SPDIF mode, the master transmitter mode is forced (MODE[1:0] = 00).</span> In Master transmitter mode, the audio block starts generating the FS and the clocks immediately.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.10 SAI configuration register 2 (SAI\_BCR2)

Address offset: 0x28

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="200.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:14</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">COMP[1:0]</span>: Companding mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. The µ-Law and the A-Law log are a part of the CCITT G.711 recommendation, the type of complement that is used depends on <span style="font-style:Italic;">CPL bit</span>.</p><p class="TableIndent">The data expansion or data compression are determined by the state of bit MODE[0]. </p><p class="TableIndent">The data compression is applied if the audio block is configured as a transmitter.</p><p class="TableIndent">The data expansion is automatically applied when the audio block is configured as a receiver.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_49327__1_13_3_Companding_Mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Companding mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: No companding algorithm</p><p class="TableIndent">01: Reserved.</p><p class="TableIndent">10: µ-Law algorithm</p><p class="TableIndent">11: A-Law algorithm</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>Companding mode is applicable only when Free protocol mode is selected.</p></td></tr><tr height="105.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 13</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">CPL</span>:<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span>Complement bit.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">It defines the type of complement to be used for companding mode </p><p class="TableIndent">0: 1’s complement representation.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: 2’s complement representation.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit has effect only when the companding mode is µ-Law algorithm or A-Law algorithm.</p></td></tr><tr height="101.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 12:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTECNT[5:0]</span>: Mute counter<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They are used only in reception mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">The value set in these bits is compared to the number of consecutive mute frames detected in reception. When the number of mute frames is equal to this value, the flag MUTEDET is set and an interrupt is generated if bit MUTEDETIE is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_54170__1_13_1_Mute_mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mute mode</a> for more details.</p></td></tr><tr height="181.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTEVAL</span>: Mute value.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only when the audio block operates as a transmitter, the number of slots is lower or equal to 2 and the MUTE bit is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">If more slots are declared, the bit value sent during the transmission in mute mode is equal to 0, whatever the value of MUTEVAL.</p><p class="TableIndent">if the number of slot is lower or equal to 2 and MUTEVAL = 1, the MUTE value transmitted for each slot is the one sent during the previous frame.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_54170__1_13_1_Mute_mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mute mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Bit value 0 is sent during the mute mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Last values are sent during the mute mode.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">This bit is meaningless and must not be used for SPDIF audio blocks.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTE:</span> Mute.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only when the audio block operates as a transmitter. The MUTE value is linked to value of MUTEVAL if the number of slots is lower or equal to 2, or equal to 0 if it is greater than 2.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_54170__1_13_1_Mute_mode20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Mute mode</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No mute mode. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: Mute mode enabled.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span><span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">This bit is meaningless and must not be used for SPDIF audio blocks.</span></p></td></tr><tr height="132.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">TRIS</span>: Tristate management on data line. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningful only if the audio block is configured as a transmitter. <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif,rm0539_mustang">This bit is not used when the audio block is configured in SPDIF mode. </span>It must be configured when SAI is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_50294__1_13_4_Ouput_data_line_management_on_an_inactive_slot20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section : Output data line management on an inactive slot</a> for more details.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: SD output line is still driven by the SAI when a slot is inactive.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: SD output line is released (HI-Z) at the end of the last data bit of the last active slot if the next one is inactive.</p></td></tr><tr height="116.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FFLUSH:</span> FIFO flush.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set by software. It is always read as 0. This bit must be configured when the SAI is disabled. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: No FIFO flush. </p><p class="TableIndent">1: FIFO flush. Programming this bit to 1 triggers the FIFO Flush. All the internal FIFO pointers (read and write) are cleared. In this case data still present in the FIFO are lost (no more transmission or received data lost). Before flushing, SAI DMA stream/interrupt must be disabled</p></td></tr><tr height="168.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 2:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FTH[2:0]:</span> FIFO threshold. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">000: FIFO empty </p><p class="TableIndent">001: ¼ FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">010: ½ FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">011: ¾ FIFO</p><p class="TableIndent">100: FIFO full</p><p class="TableIndent">101: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">110: Reserved</p><p class="TableIndent">111: Reserved</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.11 SAI frame configuration register (SAI\_BFRCR)

Address offset: 0x2C

Reset value: 0x0000 0007

**Note:**

This register has no meaning in AC’97 and SPDIF audio protocol

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 18</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSOFF</span>: Frame synchronization offset. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is meaningless and is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration. This bit must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FS is asserted on the first bit of the slot 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FS is asserted one bit before the first bit of the slot 0.</p></td></tr><tr height="102.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 17</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSPOL</span>: Frame synchronization polarity. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software. It is used to configure the level of the start of frame on the FS signal. It is meaningless and is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FS is active low (falling edge)</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FS is active high (rising edge)</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSDEF</span>: Frame synchronization definition.</p><p class="TableBodyL">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: FS signal is a start frame signal</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FS signal is a start of frame signal + channel side identification</p><p class="TableIndent">When the bit is set, the number of slots defined in the SAI_xSLOTR register has to be even. It means that half of this number of slots is dedicated to the left channel and the other slots for the right channel (e.g: this bit has to be set for I2S or MSB/LSB-justified protocols...). </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is meaningless and is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration. It must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 15</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="86.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 14:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FSALL[6:0]</span>: Frame synchronization active level length. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They specify the length in number of bit clock <br/>(SCK) + 1 (FSALL[6:0] + 1) of the active level of the FS signal in the audio frame </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are meaningless and are not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration.</p><p class="TableIndent">They must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p></td></tr><tr height="161.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FRL[7:0]</span>: Frame length. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software. They define the audio frame length expressed in number of SCK clock cycles: the number of bits in the frame is equal to FRL[7:0] + 1. </p><p class="TableIndent">The minimum number of bits to transfer in an audio frame must be equal to 8, otherwise the audio block behaves in an unexpected way. This is the case when the data size is 8 bits and only one slot 0 is defined in NBSLOT[4:0] of SAI_xSLOTR register (NBSLOT[3:0] = 0000).</p><p class="TableIndent">In master mode, if the master clock (available on MCLK_x pin) is used, the frame length must be aligned with a number equal to a power of 2, ranging from 8 to 256. When the master clock is not used (<span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3">NODIV</span> = 1), it is recommended to program the frame length to an value ranging from 8 to 256.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are meaningless and are not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>audio block configuration.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.12 SAI slot register (SAI\_BSLOTR)

Address offset: 0x30

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

**Note:**

This register has no meaning in AC’97 and SPDIF audio protocol.

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="120.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SLOTEN[15:0]</span>: Slot enable. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">Each SLOTEN bit corresponds to a slot position from 0 to 15 (maximum 16 slots).</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Inactive slot.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Active slot.</p><p class="TableIndent">The slot must be enabled when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:12</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="182.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 11:8</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">NBSLOT[3:0]</span>: Number of slots in an audio frame. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">The value set in this bitfield represents the number of slots in the audio frame (including the number of inactive slots). The maximum number of slots is 16.</p><p class="TableIndent">The number of slots must be even if FSDEF bit in the SAI_xFRCR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">The number of slots must be configured when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">0000: Number of slots is 1.</p><p class="TableIndent">0001: Number of slots is 2.</p><p class="TableIndent">...</p><p class="TableIndent">1111: Number of slots is 16.</p></td></tr><tr height="182.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 7:6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">SLOTSZ[1:0]</span>: Slot size</p><p class="TableIndent">This bits is set and cleared by software. </p><p class="TableIndent">The slot size must be higher or equal to the data size. If this condition is not respected, the behavior of the SAI is undetermined. </p><p class="TableIndent">Refer to <a href="#XREF_50294__1_13_4_Ouput_data_line_management_on_an_inactive_slot20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Output data line management on an inactive slot</a> for information on how to drive SD line. </p><p class="TableIndent">These bits must be set when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">00: The slot size is equivalent to the data size (specified in DS[3:0] in the SAI_xCR1 register).</p><p class="TableIndent">01: 16-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">10: 32-bit</p><p class="TableIndent">11: Reserved</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="117.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 4:0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FBOFF[4:0]</span>: First bit offset<span style="font-style:Italic;"> </span></p><p class="TableIndent">These bits are set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">The value set in this bitfield defines the position of the first data transfer bit in the slot. It represents an offset value. In transmission mode, the bits outside the data field are forced to 0. In reception mode, the extra received bits are discarded.</p><p class="TableIndent">These bits must be set when the audio block is disabled.</p><p class="TableIndent">They are ignored in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.13 SAI interrupt mask register (SAI\_BIM)

Address offset: 0x34

Reset value: 0x0000 0000

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LFSDETIE</span>: Late frame synchronization detection interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the LFSDET bit is set in the SAI_xSR register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is meaningless in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">, SPDIF</span> mode or when the audio block operates as a master.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">AFSDETIE</span>: Anticipated frame synchronization detection interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the AFSDET bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is meaningless in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">, SPDIF</span> mode or when the audio block operates as a master.</p></td></tr><tr height="148.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNRDYIE</span>: Codec not ready interrupt enable (AC’97). </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When the interrupt is enabled, the audio block detects in the slot 0 (tag0) of the AC’97 frame if the Codec connected to this line is ready or not. If it is not ready, the CNRDY flag in the SAI_xSR register is set and an interrupt is generated.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit has a meaning only if the AC’97 mode is selected through PRTCFG[1:0] bits and the audio block is operates as a receiver.</p></td></tr><tr height="118.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FREQIE</span>: FIFO request interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">.</span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the FREQ bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">Since the audio block defaults to operate as a transmitter after reset, the MODE bit must be configured before setting FREQIE to avoid a parasitic interrupt in receiver mode,</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WCKCFGIE</span>: Wrong clock configuration interrupt enable.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is taken into account only if the audio block is configured as a master (MODE[1] = 0) and <span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3">NODIV</span> = 0.</p><p class="TableIndent">It generates an interrupt if the WCKCFG flag in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="RegTNote">Note:<span style="padding-right:7pt ;">​</span>This bit is used only in Free protocol mode and is meaningless in other modes.</p></td></tr><tr height="104.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTEDETIE</span>: Mute detection interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">. </span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the MUTEDET bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit has a meaning only if the audio block is configured in receiver mode.</p></td></tr><tr height="88.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVRUDRIE</span>: Overrun/underrun interrupt enable<span style="font-style:Italic;">. </span></p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is set and cleared by software.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: Interrupt is disabled</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Interrupt is enabled</p><p class="TableIndent">When this bit is set, an interrupt is generated if the OVRUDR bit in the SAI_xSR register is set.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.14 SAI status register (SAI\_BSR)

Address offset: 0x38

Reset value: 0x0000 0008

<div align="left"><table class="RegBitDef"><col width="73.29px"/><col width="540.88px"/><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 31:19</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="166.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 18:16</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span class="Bold" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:Bold;font-style:normal;font-size : 9.00pt;text-decoration:none;font-kerning:normal;text-transform:none;font-stretch:normal;color:#000000;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">FLVL[2:0]</span>: FIFO level threshold. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only. The FIFO level threshold flag is managed only by hardware and its setting depends on SAI block configuration (transmitter or receiver mode). </p><p class="TableIndent">000: FIFO empty (transmitter and receiver modes)</p><p class="TableIndent">001: FIFO ≤ ¼ but not empty (transmitter mode), FIFO &lt; ¼ but not empty (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">010: ¼ &lt; FIFO ≤ ½ (transmitter mode), ¼ ≤ FIFO &lt; ½ (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">011: ½ &lt; FIFO ≤ ¾ (transmitter mode), ½ ≤ FIFO &lt; ¾ (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">100: ¾ &lt; FIFO but not full (transmitter mode), ¾ ≤ FIFO but not full (receiver mode)</p><p class="TableIndent">101: FIFO full (transmitter and receiver modes)</p><p class="TableIndent">Others: Reserved</p></td></tr><tr height="22.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bits 15:7</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL">Reserved, must be kept at reset value.</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 6</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">LFSDET</span>: Late frame synchronization detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No error.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Frame synchronization signal is not present at the right time. </p><p class="TableIndent">This flag can be set only if the audio block is configured in slave mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It is not used in AC’97 <span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if LFSDETIE bit is set in the SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets bit CLFSDET in SAI_xCLRFR register</p></td></tr><tr height="136.00px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 5</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">AFSDET</span>: Anticipated frame synchronization detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No error.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Frame synchronization signal is detected earlier than expected. </p><p class="TableIndent">This flag can be set only if the audio block is configured in slave mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It is not used in AC’97<span data-conditional="_sai1_spdif">or SPDIF </span>mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if AFSDETIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets CAFSDET bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 4</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">CNRDY</span>: Codec not ready. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: External AC’97 Codec is ready </p><p class="TableIndent">1: External AC’97 Codec is not ready</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used only when the AC’97 audio protocol is selected in the SAI_xCR1 register and configured in receiver mode.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if CNRDYIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets CCNRDY bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="169.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 3</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">FREQ</span>: FIFO request. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No FIFO request.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: FIFO request to read or to write the SAI_xDR.</p><p class="TableIndent">The request depends on the audio block configuration:</p><li class="TableBullet" id="10423457">–<span style="padding-right:3pt ;">​</span>If the block is configured in transmission mode, the FIFO request is related to a write request operation in the SAI_xDR. </li><li class="TableBullet" id="10423458">–<span style="padding-right:3pt ;">​</span>If the block configured in reception, the FIFO request related to a read request operation from the SAI_xDR.</li><p class="TableIndent">This flag can generate an interrupt if FREQIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 2</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">WCKCFG</span>: Wrong clock configuration flag. </p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only. </p><p class="TableIndent">0: Clock configuration is correct</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Clock configuration does not respect the rule concerning the frame length specification defined in <a href="#XREF_85534__1_7_Frame_synchronization20" style="font-family : Arial ; font-weight:normal;font-style:Italic;font-size : 9.00pt;font-kerning:normal;font-stretch:normal;color:#0000ff;background-color:transparent;letter-spacing : 0.00em;">Section 43.4.6: Frame synchronization</a> (configuration of FRL[7:0] bit in the SAI_xFRCR register)</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is used only when the audio block operates in master mode (MODE[1] = 0) and <span data-conditional="_sai1_ckgen1,_sai1_ckgen3">NODIV</span> = 0. </p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if WCKCFGIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets CWCKCFG bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="149.33px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 1</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">MUTEDET</span>: Mute detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No MUTE detection on the SD input line</p><p class="TableIndent">1: MUTE value detected on the SD input line (0 value) for a specified number of consecutive audio frame</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is set if consecutive 0 values are received in each slot of a given audio frame and for a consecutive number of audio frames (set in the MUTECNT bit in the SAI_xCR2 register).</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if MUTEDETIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets bit CMUTEDET in the SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr><tr height="134.67px"><td><p class="RegTBodyR">Bit 0</p></td><td><p class="TableBodyL"><span style="font-weight:Bold;">OVRUDR</span>: Overrun / underrun.</p><p class="TableIndent">This bit is read only.</p><p class="TableIndent">0: No overrun/underrun error.</p><p class="TableIndent">1: Overrun/underrun error detection.</p><p class="TableIndent">The overrun and underrun conditions can occur only when the audio block is configured as a receiver and a transmitter, respectively.</p><p class="TableIndent">It can generate an interrupt if OVRUDRIE bit is set in SAI_xIM register.</p><p class="TableIndent">This flag is cleared when the software sets COVRUDR bit in SAI_xCLRFR register.</p></td></tr></table></div>


### 43.6.15 SAI clear flag register (SAI\_BCLRFR)

Address offset: 0x3C

Reset value: 0x0000 0000
